Appendix B
Proof of Main Results

B.1
Semi-supervised
Q
-learning asymptotics

In this section we first show the proofs for the theoretical results on the generalized semi-supervised
Q
-learning shown in section
5
.

B.1.1
Proofs for theoretical results for
Q
-learning in section
5

We first define
\btheta_{2-}\equiv(\bbeta_{22}\trans,\bgamma_{2}\trans)\trans
, and
\hat{\Delta}_{s}\supnk(\bUvec)\equiv\mhat_{s}\supnk(\bUvec)-m_{s}(\bUvec)
,
s\in\{2,3,22,23\}
, and note that from Assumptions
5.1
,
5.1
&
5.1
it follows that:

\begin{split}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}&\sup\limits_{\bUvec}\left|\hat{\Delta}_{2t}\supnk(\bUvec)\right|=o_{\Pbb}(1)\text{ for }t=2,3,\\
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}&\sup\limits_{\bXvec,\bUvec}\|\bXvec\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\|=o_{\Pbb}(1),\\
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}&\sup\limits_{,\bX_{2}\bUvec}\|\bX_{2}\hat{\Delta}_{3}\supnk(\bUvec)\|=o_{\Pbb}(1),\end{split}

(8)

Next we remind that, to ensure the validity of the SSL algorithm from the refitted imputation model, the final imputation models for
\{Y_{t},Y_{2t},t=2,3\}
, denoted by
\{\mubar_{t}(\bUvec),\mubar_{2t},t=2,3\}
, need to satisfy the constraints shown in Section
3.2
:

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bXvec\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left\{Y_{2}^{2}-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)\right\}=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle,

(9)

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bX_{2}\{Y_{3}-\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left\{Y_{2}Y_{3}-\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)\right\}=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle.

where
\bXvec=(1,\bX_{1}\trans,\bX_{2}\trans)\trans
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Recall the estimating equation for stage 2 regression in Section
3.2
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pbb_{N}&\begin{bmatrix}\muhat_{23}(\bUvec)-\hat{\beta}_{21}\muhat_{22}(\bUvec)-\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetahat_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\muhat_{3}(\bUvec)-\hat{\beta}_{21}\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\bX_{2}\trans\bthetahat_{2-}\right\}\end{bmatrix}=\textbf{0}.\end{split}

Centering the above at
\bthetabar_{2}
we get

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\muhat_{22}(\bUvec),\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\\
\bX_{2}\muhat_{2}(\bUvec),\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\end{bmatrix}(\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2})=\Pbb_{N}

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\muhat_{23}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\muhat_{22}(\bUvec)-\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\muhat_{3}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\right\}\end{bmatrix}.

(10)

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)-\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\right\}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\left\{\muhat_{23}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\left\{\muhat_{22}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)\right\}-\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\muhat_{3}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bX_{2}\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)&\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\\
\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}&\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\muhat_{22}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)&\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}\trans\\
\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}&\bzero\\
\end{bmatrix},

with these we can re-write equation (
10
) as

\left(\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}}+\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}\right)(\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2})=\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}}+\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}
. We next deal with each term.

(I) We first consider
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}
, let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\left(\etahat_{23}-\eta_{23}\right)-\bar{\beta}_{21}(\etahat_{22}-\eta_{22})-(\bEtahat_{2}-\bEta_{2})\trans\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\left\{\left(\bEtahat_{3}-\bEta_{3}\right)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\left(\bEtahat_{2}-\bEta_{2}\right)\right\}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{23}\supnk(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec)-\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\hat{\Delta}_{3}\supnk(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\right\}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{23}\supnk(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec)-\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{\hat{\Delta}_{3}\supnk(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\right\}\end{bmatrix}\>\text{ for }k\in\{1,\dots,K\}.

From (
3
) it follows that

\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}+\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}.

Next using (
8
), Assumption
5.1
, and Lemma
15
it follows that
\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, which lets us write
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
.

Now consider
\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}
, note that by the central limit theorem (CLT)
\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\bX_{2}=\Ebb\bX_{2}\bX_{2}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Thus using this, Slutsky’s theorem and Assumption
5.1

(\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\bX_{2})^{-1}(\Pbb_{N}\bX_{2}\bX_{2})=I+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

then using (
9
), (
3
) and Assumption
5.1
we can write

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left\{(\bEtahat_{2}-\bEta_{2})\trans\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\left(\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{2i}\left\{Y_{2i}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec_{i})+m_{2}(\bUvec_{i})-m_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})\right\}\right]\trans\Pbb_{N}(\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans)\btheta_{2-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\trans\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{2i}\trans\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})\right]\left(\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\right)^{-1}\Pbb_{N}(\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans)\btheta_{2-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\btheta_{2-}\trans\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{2i}\trans\btheta_{2-}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left[\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\btheta_{2-}\trans\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{2i}\trans\btheta_{2-}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\bX_{2}\btheta_{2-}\trans\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\sum\limits_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{2i}\trans\btheta_{2-}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right)+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)o_{\Pbb}(1).

Analogous derivations for all terms in
\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}
gives us

\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}-\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}^{(K)}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right)+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)o_{\Pbb}(1),

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\begin{bmatrix}\left\{Y_{2}Y_{3}-\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\left\{Y_{2}^{2}-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)\right\}-\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\left\{Y_{3}-\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bX_{2}\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}^{(K)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{23}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})-\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})\bX_{2i}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2i}\left\{\hat{\Delta}_{3}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})-\bar{\beta}_{21}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})\right\}\end{bmatrix}.

From the above it follows that
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}-\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}^{(K)}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right)+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)o_{\Pbb}(1)
. Next by Assumption
5.1
and using Lemma
16
with
\hat{C}_{n,N}=1
, and setting functions
\hat{l}_{n}(\cdot)
,
\hat{\pi}_{n}(\cdot)
to be the constant 1, and
f(\bX_{2})=\bX_{2}
to be the identity function, we have
\sqrt{n}\left(\mathbb{T}^{(K)}_{\mathcal{L}}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{k}\right)=O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)
. Therefore
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)
.

(II) Now we consider
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}}
, from the CLT, assuming working model (
1
), as constraints (
9
) are satisfied it follows that

\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{U}}=\mathbb{E}\begin{bmatrix}\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\\
\bX_{2}\{\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)-\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\}\end{bmatrix}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)=\boldsymbol{1}O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(III) Next we focus on
\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}
, we use a similar expansion to (I) and define

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\eta}=

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\etahat_{22}-\eta_{22}&(\etahat_{2}-\eta_{2})\bX_{2}\trans\\
\left(\etahat_{2}-\eta_{2}\right)\bX_{2}&\bzero\\
\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(K)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Pbb_{N}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec)&\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans&\\
\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}&\bzero\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{k}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec)&\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans&\\
\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}&\bzero\end{bmatrix}\>\forall k\in\{1,\dots,K\},

We argue as in (I), that from (
3
) it follows that

\hat{\Gamma}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}+\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}.
Using (
8
), Assumptions
5.1
and Lemma
15

\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{k}=O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, therefore
\hat{\Gamma}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Next we follow the same decomposition for
\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}
as we did in (I) for
\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{\eta}_{\mathbb{S}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\begin{bmatrix}Y_{2}^{2}-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)&\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}\trans\\
\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\bX_{2}&\bzero\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\Delta}_{22}\supnk(\bUvec)&\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans&\\
\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\bX_{2}&\bzero\end{bmatrix}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right)+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)o_{\Pbb}(1).

The first term in the right hand side is
O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
by he CLT, the next two terms together are
O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)
by Lemma
16
, thus
\hat{\Gamma}^{(K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right).

(IV) Finally we consider
\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}}
. By central limit theorem and (
9
) it follows that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}}=\mathbb{E}\begin{bmatrix}\mubar_{22}(\bUvec)&\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\\
\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}&\bX_{2}\bX_{2}\trans\\
\end{bmatrix}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)=\mathbb{E}[\bXcheck_{2}\bXcheck_{2}\trans]+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

From (I)-(IV) we can write (
10
) as
(\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2})=\mathbb{E}\left[\bXcheck_{2}\bXcheck_{2}\trans\right]^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{L}}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bXcheck_{2}\bXcheck_{2}\trans\right]^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\begin{bmatrix}\left\{Y_{2i}Y_{3i}-\mubar_{23}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\left\{Y_{2i}^{2}-\mubar_{22}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}-\bXcheck_{2i}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\left\{Y_{2i}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}\\
\bX_{2i}\left\{Y_{3i}-\mubar_{3}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bX_{2i}\left\{Y_{2i}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right).

∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The solution to stage 1 estimating equation
\btheta_{1}
in Section
3.2
satisfies

\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)+\hat{\beta}_{21}\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{22}+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetahat_{1}\right\}\right]=\bzero.

We center the above at
\bthetabar_{1}
and get

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\bX_{1}\trans\right]\left(\bthetahat_{1}-\bthetabar_{1}\right)=\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\hat{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{22}+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\right\}\right].

(11)

Next, with the following definitions

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}_{\Usc}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\bX_{1}\trans\right],\quad\hat{\Sigma}_{\Lsc}=\Pbb_{n}\left[\bX_{1}\bX_{1}\trans\right],

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\hat{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{22}+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{(1K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\muhat_{2}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\right],

we can write (
11
) as
\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}(\bthetahat_{1}-\bthetabar_{1})=\mathcal{R}^{(1)}+(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{(1K)}_{\mathbb{S}}
. We now analyze both terms
\mathcal{R}^{(1)},
and
(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{(1K)}_{\mathbb{S}}
.

I) First we consider

, define

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(1\mathbb{K})}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{(1)}_{k}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\right],

from (
3
) it follows that

, next from Assumptions
5.1
,
5.1
, we get
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sup\limits_{\bX_{1},\bUvec}\|\bX_{1}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\|=o_{\Pbb}(1)
, thus by Lemma

we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(1\eta)}_{\mathbb{S}}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bX_{1i}\left\{Y_{2i}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{1i}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

where the last line follows by the CLT and Assumptions
5.1
and
5.1
as

\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{U}}\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}=I+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

Now using Lemma
15
and Assumptions
5.1
,
5.1
again, it follows that

\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{(1\mathbb{K})}_{\mathbb{S}}=\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{(1)}_{k}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

combining the above we can write

\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(1K)}=\Pbb_{n}\bX_{1}\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\bX_{1i}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec_{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\hat{\Delta}_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)\right]\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

we have

therefore
\hat{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{(1K)}=\Pbb_{n}\bX_{1}\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)
. Finally using Theorem

we have

, and by CLT
\Pbb_{n}\bX_{1}\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, thus we can write

(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{(1K)}_{\mathbb{S}}=(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{n}\bX_{1}\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right).

II) Next we consider
\mathcal{R}^{(1)}
by writing

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{22}+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)(\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21})+\bH_{20}\trans(\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetabar_{22})+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right\}\right],

) using model (

) the first term in the right hand side is mean zero, therefore from Assumption

\Pbb_{N}\left\{\bX_{1}\left(\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bbetabar_{21}\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{22}+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\right)\right\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mathcal{R}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)(\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21})+\bH_{20}\trans(\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetabar_{22})+\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right\}\right]+O_{\Pbb}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left(\mubar_{2}(\bUvec),\bH_{20}\trans\right)\right]\sqrt{n}\left(\bbetahat_{2}-\bbetabar_{2}\right)+\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)\right]+O_{\Pbb}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\left(\mubar_{2}(\bUvec),\bH_{20}\trans\right)\right]n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi_{2i\beta}+\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\bX_{1}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)\right]+O_{\Pbb}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{N}}\right),

where the last inequality follows from the CLT, where

is the element corresponding to
\bbetahat_{2}
of the influence function
\bpsi_{2i}
defined in Theorem

.

Next by Theorem

we know that

\sqrt{n}(\bgammahat_{2}-\bgammabar_{2})=O_{\Pbb}(1),

using Lemma

(a) we have

\Pbb\left[\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\bX_{1}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)\right\}=\Pbb_{N}\bigg\{\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans I\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\bigg\}\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat_{2}-\bgammabar_{2}\right)\right]\rightarrow 1.

Therefore, letting
\bpsi_{2i\gamma}
be the element corresponding to
\bgammahat_{2}
of the influence function
\bpsi_{2i}
defined in Theorem

,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\bX_{1}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Pbb_{N}\left\{\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)I\left(\bgammahat_{2}\in\mathcal{A}\right)\right\}\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat_{2}-\bgammabar_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\bX_{1}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)\right\}I_{\left\{\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans|\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0,\bgammahat_{2}\in\mathcal{A}\right]\Pbb\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\Pbb\left(\bgammahat_{2}\in\mathcal{A}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{2\gamma_{2}i}+O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right)+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans|\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right]\Pbb\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{2\gamma_{2}i}+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right),

combining all terms

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mathcal{R}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n^{-}_{K}}\right).

by the LLN, we have

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right),

using (

) we have

which yields our required results

Next we discuss some results and assumptions needed for Proposition
5
. First we show the asymptotic results for the supervised estimation of the
Q
-function parameters. Recall
\bthetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP},

\bthetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
are the estimators for the
Q
-function parameters, when using the labeled data
\Lsc
only. From
Laber et al. 2014
we have that the following results for
\bthetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
:

\sqrt{n}\left(\bthetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-\bthetabar_{2}\right)=\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\rightarrow\Nsc\left(\bzero,\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left[\bthetabar_{2}\right]\right),

with

\displaystyle\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})

\displaystyle=\bXcheck_{2}\{Y_{3i}-\bXcheck_{2i}\trans\bthetabar_{2}\},

\displaystyle\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left[\bthetabar_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans,

and for
\bthetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
:

\sqrt{n}\left(\bthetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-\bthetabar_{1}\right)=\Sigma_{1}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})\rightarrow\Nsc\left(\bzero,\bV_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left[\bthetabar_{1}\right]\right),

with

\displaystyle\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL_{i};\bthetabar_{2})=

\displaystyle\bX_{i1}\{Y_{2i}+Y_{2i}\bar{\beta}_{21}+\bH_{20i}\trans\bbetabar_{22}+[\bH_{21i}\trans\bgammabar_{2}]_{+}-\bX_{1i}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\left(Y_{2},\bH_{20}\trans\right)\right]\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP,(\beta)}(\bL_{i})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans|\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right]\Pbb\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP,(\gamma)}(\bL_{i}),

\displaystyle\bV_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left[\bthetabar_{1}\right]=

\displaystyle\bSigma_{1}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{1}^{-1}\right)\trans.

Next we discuss the assumption required for Proposition
5
. We need the imputation models
\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)
,
s\in\{2,3,22,23\}
to satisfy several additional constraints. For example, for the stage two
Q
-function parameters, recall
\btheta_{2-}=(\bbeta_{22}\trans,\bgamma_{2}\trans)\trans
, the imputation models should satisfy:

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bXvec\trans\mubar_{j}(\bUvec)\{g_{s}(\bY)-\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bXvec\trans\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\{g_{s}(\bY)-\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero

\displaystyle,s,j\in\{2,3,22,23\}

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bXvec\bXvec\trans\mubar_{j}(\bUvec)\{g_{s}(\bY)-\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\bXvec\bXvec\trans\bX_{2}\trans\bthetabar_{2-}\{g_{s}(\bY)-\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)\}\right]=\bzero

\displaystyle,s,j\in\{2,3\},

where
\bX=(1,\bX_{1}\trans,\bX_{2}\trans)\trans
,
g_{2}(\bY)=Y_{2},

g_{3}(\bY)=Y_{3},

g_{22}(\bY)=Y_{2}^{2},

g_{23}(\bY)=Y_{2}Y_{3}
.

To summarize all the assumptions needed, we define the following functions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec)\equiv&\left\{\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec)\trans,\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\trans\right\}\trans,\\
\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\equiv&\begin{bmatrix}\mubar_{23}(\bUvec)-[\mubar_{22}(\bUvec),\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bX_{2}\trans]\bthetabar_{2}\\
\bX_{2}\{\mubar_{3}(\bUvec)-[\mubar_{2}(\bUvec),\bX_{2}\trans]\bthetabar_{2}\}\end{bmatrix},\\
\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec)\equiv&\bX_{1}\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})+\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\}\\
+&\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\left(Y_{2},\bH_{20}\trans\right)\right]\mathcal{E}_{2\beta}(\bUvec)\\
+&\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans|\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right]\Pbb\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\mathcal{E}_{2\gamma}(\bUvec),\end{split}

(12)

where
\mathcal{E}_{2\beta}(\bUvec)
,
\mathcal{E}_{2\gamma}(\bUvec)
are the elements corresponding to
\bbetabar_{2}
,
\bgammabar_{2}
of
\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)
. Now we can succinctly summarize the constraints, by having
\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)
,
s\in\{2,3,22,23\}
satisfy

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\trans\right]=\bzero.

This is condensed in the following assumption.

{assumption}

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec)
be as defined in (
12
), and

\bpsi\subSUP(\bL;\bthetabar)=\left[\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})\trans,\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\trans\right]\trans,

the imputation models
\mubar_{s}(\bUvec)
,
s\in\{2,3,22,23\}
satisfy

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\bpsi\subSUP(\bL;\bthetabar)-\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec)\right\}\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec)\trans\right]=\bzero.

Proof of Proposition
5
.

We first show the result is true for
\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}\left[\bthetabar_{2}\right]
. To simplify algebra, we denote the influence function from Theorem
2
as
\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})
. Using the influence function of
\bthetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
and Theorem
2
we have the following relationship:

\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})=\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec).

Therefore

\displaystyle\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}\left(\bthetabar_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\left\{\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\left\{\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\Ebb\left[\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\trans\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans

Now, since our imputation models satisfy Assumption
B.1.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\bpsi_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{2})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\right\}\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\trans\right]=\bzero.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}\left(\bthetabar_{2}\right)=

\displaystyle\bV_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left(\bthetabar_{2}\right)-\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\text{Var}\left[\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)\right]\left(\bSigma_{2}^{-1}\right)\trans.

To show the result is true for
\bV_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}\left[\bthetabar_{1}\right]
,
We denote by
\mathcal{E}_{2\beta}(\bUvec)
and
\mathcal{E}_{2\gamma}(\bUvec)
the vectors corresponding to
\bbetabar_{2}
,
\bgammabar_{2}
in
\mathcal{E}_{2}(\bUvec)
respectively, and further recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec)=

\displaystyle\bX_{1}\{\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)+\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\bar{\beta}_{21}+\bH_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{22}+[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}]_{+}-\bX_{1}\trans\bthetabar_{1}\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\left(Y_{2},\bH_{20}\trans\right)\right]\mathcal{E}_{2\beta}(\bUvec)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bX_{1}\bH_{21}\trans|\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right]\Pbb\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0\right)\mathcal{E}_{2\gamma}(\bUvec).

From the form of the influence function of
\bthetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
, and Theorems
2
&
3
we have that:

\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})=\bpsi_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}(\bL;\bthetabar_{1})-\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec).

Analogous steps for the proof of
\bthetabar_{2}
can then be used to show

\displaystyle\bV_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SSL}\left(\bthetabar_{1}\right)=

\displaystyle\bV_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\left(\bthetabar_{1}\right)-\bSigma_{1}^{-1}\text{Var}\left[\mathcal{E}_{1}(\bUvec)\right]\left(\bSigma_{1}^{-1}\right)\trans.

The required result is obtained by stacking the influence functions for
\btheta_{1},\btheta_{2}
for the supervised and semi-supervised versions, noting that

\bpsi\subSSL(\bL;\bthetabar)=\bpsi\subSUP(\bL;\bthetabar)-\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec).

and repeating the steps above.
∎

B.2
Value Function Results

In this Section we prove the main results for our SSL value function estimator. Before the proofs we go over some useful definitions, notation and lemmas. First recall that, in order to correct for potential biases arising from finite sample estimation and model mis-specifications, the final imputed models for
\{Y_{2},\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar),

Y_{t}\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar),

t=2,3\}
satisfy the following constraints:

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar)\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

(13)

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\Qopt_{2-}(\bUvec;\btheta_{2})\left\{\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar)-\mubar_{\omega_{2}}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar)Y_{t}-\mubar^{v}_{t\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right]

\displaystyle=0,\>t=2,3.

Next, define the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\delta)=\bigg\{(\btheta,\bxi)\bigg|\|\bthetahat-\btheta\|_{2}^{2}<\delta,\|\bxihat-{\bxi}\|_{2}^{2}<\delta,\btheta_{t}\in\Theta_{t},{\bxi}_{t}\in\Omega_{t},t=1,2,

\displaystyle\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};{\bxi}_{1})>0,\>\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};{\bxi}_{2})>0,\>\forall\>\bH\in\mathcal{H}\bigg\}.

We will be using the influence functions for our model parameters
\bTheta
. In this regard let
\bpsi^{\theta}=(\bpsi_{1}\trans,\bpsi_{2}\trans)\trans
. By Theorems
2
&
3

\sqrt{n}(\bthetahat-\bthetabar)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi^{\theta}(\bUvec_{i})+o_{\Pbb}(1)
. Next, from Assumption
5.2
, it can be shown that
\bxihat
has the following expansion:

\sqrt{n}(\bxihat-\bxibar)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi^{\xi}\left(\bL_{i};\bxibar\right)+o_{\Pbb}(1),

where

\bpsi_{t}^{\xi}\left(\bL;\bxibar\right)=\Ebb\left\{\bHcheck_{t}\trans\bHcheck_{t}\sigma\left(\bHcheck_{t}\trans\bxibar_{t}\right)[1-\sigma\left(\bHcheck_{t}\trans\bxibar_{t}\right)]\right\}^{-1}\bHcheck_{t}\left\{A_{t}-\sigma\left(\bHcheck_{t}\trans\bxibar_{t}\right)\right\},\>\>t=1,2,

\bpsi^{\xi}\left(\bL;\bxibar\right)=\left[\bpsi_{1}^{\xi}\left(\bL;\bxibar\right),\bpsi_{2}^{\xi}\left(\bL;\bxibar\right)\right]
and
\mathbb{E}[\bpsi^{\xi}]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[(\bpsi^{\xi})\trans\bpsi^{\xi}]<\infty
.

We now introduce a set of definitions used in this section to make the proofs easier to read. Recall from (
7
) we have

\Vhat\subSSLDR=\Pbb_{N}\left\{\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetahat,\muhat)\right\},

where
\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetahat,\muhat)
is the semi-supervised augmented estimator for observation
\bUvec
, we re-write
\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetahat,\muhat)
as
\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\muhat}(\bUvec)
recall its definition, and define the following functions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\muhat}(\bUvec)\equiv&\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetahat_{1})+\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bThetahat)\left[(1+\betahat_{21})\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetahat_{1})+\Qopt_{2-}\{\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2}\}\right]\\
+&\muhat^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\betahat_{21}\muhat^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})\muhat^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),\\
\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\muhat}(\bUvec)\equiv&\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetabar_{1})+\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bThetabar)\left[(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetabar_{1})+\Qopt_{2-}\{\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2}\}\right]\\
+&\muhat^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\muhat^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\muhat^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec).\end{split}

(14)

We next replace the estimated imputation functions with their limits
\mubar^{v}_{2}
,
\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}
,
\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}
and
\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}
, and define:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{V}_{\bThetahat,\mubar}(\bUvec)\equiv&\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetahat_{1})+\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bThetahat)\left[(1+\betahat_{21})\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetahat_{1})+\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})\right]\\
+&\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\hat{\beta}_{21}\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),\\
\mathcal{V}_{\bThetabar,\mubar}(\bUvec)\equiv&\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetabar_{1})+\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bThetabar)\left[(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetabar_{1})+\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\right]\\
+&\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\bar{\beta}_{21}\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec).\end{split}

(15)

Finally we define the following functions which are weighted sums of the imputation function errors:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}(\bUvec)\equiv&\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat)(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\left\{\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}+\muhat^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\\
-&\hat{\beta}_{21}\left\{\muhat^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\}-\Qopt_{2-}\left(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2}\right)\left\{\muhat^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\},\\
\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}(\bUvec)\equiv&\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar)(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\left\{\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}+\muhat^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\\
-&\bar{\beta}_{21}\left\{\muhat^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\}-\Qopt_{2-}\left(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2}\right)\left\{\muhat^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\}.\end{split}

(16)

These definitions will come in handy in the following proofs as we can use them to write
\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\muhat}(\bUvec)=\mathcal{V}_{\bThetahat,\mubar}(\bUvec)+\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}(\bUvec)
,
\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\muhat}(\bUvec)=\mathcal{V}_{\bThetabar,\mubar}(\bUvec)+\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}(\bUvec)
. Finally, recalling that
\Pbb_{\bUvec}
is the underlying distribution of the data, we define function
g_{1}:\bTheta\mapsto\mathbb{R}
as

g_{1}(\bTheta)=\int\Vsc_{\bTheta,\mubar}(\bUvec)d\Pbb_{\bUvec}.

With the above definitions we proceed by stating three lemmas that will be used to prove Theorem
7
. We defer the proofs of these lemmas for after proving the main Theorem in this section.

Lemma 11
.

Under Assumptions
5.1
-
5.2
, we have

I)

\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}\left(\bThetabar\right)\right\}=o_{\Pbb}(1),

II)

\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{n}\left\{g_{1}(\bThetahat)-g_{1}\left(\bThetabar\right)\right\}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}g_{1}\left(\bThetabar\right)\right)\trans\bpsi^{\theta}(\bUvec_{i})+\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}g_{1}\left(\bThetabar\right)\right)\trans\bpsi^{\xi}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}+o_{\Pbb}(1).

Lemma 12
.

Under Assumptions
5.1
-
5.2
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\bigg(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetahat)\bigg)-\bigg(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\bigg)\right\}=o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right).

Lemma 13
.

Under Assumptions
5.1
-
5.2
, the following assertions hold:

I)

\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}-\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right\}=o_{\Pbb}(1),

II)

\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\bpsi^{\theta}(\bL_{i})\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\int\nu\subSSLDR(\bL_{i};\bTheta)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}+\bpsi^{\xi}(\bL_{i})\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\int\nu\subSSLDR(\bL_{i};\bTheta)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt+o_{\Pbb}(1).

Proof of Theorem
7
.

We start by expanding the expression in (
7
) and using definitions (
14
), (
15
), (
16
):

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\muhat}\right]-\Ebb_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\underbrace{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]+\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]}_{(I)}-\underbrace{g_{1}(\bThetabar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]}_{(II)}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\bigg(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}\right]+\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}\right]-\underbrace{g_{1}(\bThetahat)\bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}\right]}_{(III)}\right\}-\left\{\underbrace{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]+\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]}_{(I)}-\underbrace{g_{1}(\bThetabar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]}_{(II)}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\underbrace{g_{1}(\bThetahat)+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}\right]}_{(III)}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{g_{1}(\bThetahat)-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\left(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetahat)\right)-\bigg(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}-\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi^{v}_{\subSSLDR}(\bL_{i};\bThetabar)+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right).

which follows from Lemmas
11
,
12
&
13
with the influence function
\psi^{v}_{\subSSLDR}
defined as

\displaystyle\psi^{v}\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetabar)=

\displaystyle\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetabar)+\bpsi^{\theta}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\int\left\{\Vsc_{\bTheta,\mubar}(\bL)+\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bTheta)\right\}d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}

\displaystyle\hskip 59.75095pt+\bpsi^{\xi}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\int\left\{\Vsc_{\bTheta,\mubar}(\bUvec)+\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bTheta)\right\}d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar},

\displaystyle\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)=

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\left\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}+\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bThetabar_{2})Y_{3}-\mubar_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\bar{\beta}_{21}\left\{\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bThetabar_{2})Y_{2}-\mubar_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\}-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\left\{\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bThetabar_{2})-\mubar_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right\}

Next note that

\int\left(\Vsc_{\bTheta,\mubar}(\bUvec)+\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bTheta)\right)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}=\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bTheta)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar},

where
\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bTheta)
is defined in (
5
).
Finally, all random variables in the expression of
\psi^{v}_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)
are bounded by Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{v}_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)^{2}\right]<\infty
, the central limit theorem yields that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\muhat}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\right\}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi^{v}_{\subSSLDR}(\bL_{i};\bThetabar)+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N\left(0,\sigma^{2}\subSSLDR\right).

∎

Proof of Lemma
11
.

I) We start with
\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\right\}
. Note that
\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}(\bUvec)
is a deterministic function of random variable
\bUvec
as parameters and imputation functions are fixed.
We have that
\Ebb\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}(\bUvec)^{2}\right]<\infty
holds by Assumption
5.1
&
5.2
. Thus the central limit theorem yields
\mathbb{G}_{N}\left\{\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,Var\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]\right),
therefore

\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\right\}=\sqrt{\frac{n}{N}}\mathbb{G}_{N}\left\{\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{N}\right)=o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right).

Proof of Lemma

.

We consider
\sqrt{n}\left\{\left(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetahat)\right)-\bigg(\Pbb_{N}\left[\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right]-g_{1}(\bThetabar)\bigg)\right\}
, recall that
d_{t}(\bHcheck_{t},\btheta_{t})=I(\bH_{t1}\trans\bgamma_{t}>0)

, thus the inverse probability weight functions are defined as

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1}>0)A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{\{1-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1}>0)\}\{1-A_{1}\}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})},\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)

\displaystyle\equiv\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left(\frac{I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2}>0)A_{2}}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{\{1-I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2}>0)\}\{1-A_{2}\}}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right).

Define the class

\ell_{t}=\{I\left(\bH_{t}\trans\bgamma_{t}\geq 0\right):\mathcal{H}_{t1},\bgamma\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{t}}\},\>t=1,2

and the collection of half spaces
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}\equiv\left\{\bH_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{t}}:\bH_{t}\trans\bgamma_{t}\geq 0,\bgamma\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{t}},t\in\{1,2\}\right\}
. By
Dudley 1979

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
is a VC class of VC dimension
q_{t}+1
. Next by
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
we have that as
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
is a VC-class
\ell_{t}
is a class of the same index. Finally, by Theorem 2.6.7 we have that
\ell_{t}
is a
\Pbb
-Donsker class.
Next define the following function

\displaystyle f_{\bTheta}(\bUvec)=

\displaystyle\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})+\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left[(1+\beta_{21})\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})+\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\btheta_{2})\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mubar^{v}_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\beta_{21}\mubar^{v}_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\btheta_{2})\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec).

We define the associated class of functions
\mathcal{C}_{1}=\left\{f_{\bTheta}(\bUvec)|\bUvec,\bTheta\in\mathcal{S}(\delta)\right\}.

i) By Assumptions
5.1
,
5.2
and

Vaart 1998
,
\ell_{t},\>\mathcal{W}_{t},\>\mathcal{Q}_{t},t=1,2
are
\Pbb
-Donsker classes. Thus it follows that
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is a Donsker class.

ii) We estimate
{\bxi}_{1},{\bxi}_{2}
for (
4

\bxihat_{1},\bxihat_{2}
, solving

iii) We next show
\int\left(\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}-\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}\longrightarrow 0.

By Assumptions
5.2
(ii),
5.2
, and bounded covariates and there exists a constant

such that we can write

\displaystyle\int\left(\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}-\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\bigg(\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\bthetahat_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\bthetabar_{1})\bigg)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\int\bigg(\frac{1}{1-\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{1-\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\bigg)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\int\left(\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\int\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bHcheck_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\int\left\{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}>0)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\int\left(\bH_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{22}+[\bH_{20}\trans\bgammabar_{2}]_{+}-\bH_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{22}-[\bH_{20}\trans\bgammahat_{2}]_{+}\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

where we use
(a-b)^{2},(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}\>\forall a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
\dhat_{1},A_{1}\leq 1
for all
\bH\in\mathcal{H}
, and boundedness of
\bthetahat_{t},t=1,2
by Assumptions
5.1
-
5.1
. Next note that all terms outside integrals are bounded by Assumptions
5.1
-
5.1
. Finally we consider terms within the integrals with the following example

\displaystyle\int\left(\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar)\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}=

\displaystyle\int\left(\bH_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{22}+[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}]_{+}-\bH_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{22}-[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}]_{+}\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\|\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetabar_{22}\|_{2}^{2}\int\bH_{20}\trans\bH_{20}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}

\displaystyle+

which follows from Theorem

and Lemma

(a). All similar terms can be handled accordingly. We get the convergence in probability to 0:
\int\left(\Vsc_{\bThetahat,\mubar}-\Vsc_{\bThetabar,\mubar}\right)^{2}d\Pbb_{\bUvec}\rightarrow 0
as all other terms within expectation are

by the dominating convergence theorem, boundedness

,

, and the consistency of
\bxihat

is a Donsker class, and

Proof of Lemma
13
.

I) First note that from the empirical normal equations (
6
), we have that the solution
\etahat_{2}^{v}
satisfies
\etahat_{2}^{v}-\eta_{2}^{v}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Therefore

\displaystyle\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\mu_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\frac{1}{K}\mhat_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)+\etahat_{2}^{v}-m_{2}(\bUvec)+\eta_{2}^{v}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K}\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\mhat_{2}\supnk(\bUvec)+m_{2}(\bUvec)\right|+\left|\etahat_{2}^{v}-\eta_{2}^{v}\right|

\displaystyle=o_{\Pbb}(1)+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)=o_{\Pbb}(1),

where we additionally use Assumption
5.2
for the difference of estimated and true imputation models
\hat{m}_{2}
,
m_{2}
. Similarly
\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat^{v}_{t\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{t\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right|=o_{\Pbb}(1)
,
\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right|=o_{\Pbb}(1)
,
t=2,3
. Next, using the triangle and Jensen’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}-\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})-\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\bigg|\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec)\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg|\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bigg|\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mu_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\bigg|\sup_{\bUvec}\left|\muhat_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)-\mubar_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})-\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\bigg|o_{\Pbb}(1)+\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})\hat{\beta}_{21}-\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\bar{\beta}_{21}\bigg|o_{\Pbb}(1)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg|\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bigg|o_{\Pbb}(1)+\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\left(\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetahat_{22}\right)\trans\bH_{20}+[\bgammahat_{2}\trans\bH_{21}]_{+}-[\bgammabar_{2}\trans\bH_{21}]_{+}\bigg|o_{\Pbb}(1).

By Theorem
2
we have
\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, also from Lemma

(a) it follows that

, hence as covariates are bounded we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\bigg|o_{\Pbb}(1)+\Pbb_{N}\bigg|\left(\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetahat_{22}\right)\trans\bH_{20}+[\bgammahat_{2}\trans\bH_{21}]_{+}-[\bgammabar_{2}\trans\bH_{21}]_{+}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{o_{\Pbb}(1)+\sup_{\bH_{20}}\|\bH_{20}\|_{2}\|\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetabar_{22}\|_{2}+\sup_{\bH_{21}}\|\bH_{21}\|_{2}\right\}O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Next, we can write

\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})=I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)\right\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}\right\}.

By Lemma
17

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)\right\}-I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left[\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Using the above and Lemma

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}_{21}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})\right\}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})\right\}-\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

From the above we get

\Pbb_{N}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{\bThetahat}-\mathcal{E}_{\bThetabar}\right\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)o_{\Pbb}(1).

II) To show the relevant result, we first recall the definition of

&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Ebb\left[\nu\subSSLDR(\bL_{i};\bThetabar)\right]\right)\trans\bpsi^{\theta}(\bL_{i})\\

(17)

2) We next show

define the class

and the collection of half spaces
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}\equiv\left\{\bH_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{t}}:\bH\trans\bgamma_{t}\geq 0,\bgamma\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{t}},t\in\{1,2\}\right\}

\displaystyle f_{\bTheta}(\bL_{i})=

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1i},A_{1i};\bTheta_{1})(1+\beta_{21})\left\{Y_{2i}-\mubar_{2}^{v}(\bUvec_{i})\right\}+\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2i},A_{2i};\bTheta_{2})Y_{3i}-\mubar_{3\omega_{2}}(\bUvec_{i})

iii) We then show

\displaystyle\int\left\{\nu\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetahat)-\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bL}

\displaystyle\sup_{\bHcheck_{1},A_{1}}\left\{(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})-(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{Y_{2}}Y_{2}^{2}\sup_{\bHcheck_{2},A_{2}}\left\{\hat{\beta}_{21}\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetahat_{2})-\bar{\beta}_{21}\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar_{2})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bUvec}\mubar_{2\omega_{3}}(\bUvec)^{2}\left(\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bHcheck_{2},A_{2}}\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetabar_{2})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bUvec}\mubar_{2\omega_{2}}(\bUvec)^{2}\sup_{\bH_{2}}\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}0

where we use
(a-b)^{2},(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}\>\forall a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, boundedness of
\bThetabar
and covariates by Assumptions

,
5.1
to bound all supremum quantities.

By Theorems

and

we have
\bthetahat_{2}-\bthetabar_{2}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
,
\bthetahat_{1}-\bthetabar_{1}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, also from Lemma

(a) it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{2}}\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\bH_{20}}\|\bH_{20}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bbetahat_{22}-\bbetabar_{22}\|_{2}^{2}+2\sup_{\bH_{21}}\|\bH_{21}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bgammahat_{22}-\bgammabar_{22}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right).

Next, we can write

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})=

\displaystyle I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)\right\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}\right\}

\displaystyle\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bThetahat_{1})=

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})I\left\{A_{2}=d_{2}\left(\bH_{2};\bxihat_{2}\right)\right\}\left\{\frac{A_{2}}{\pi_{2}\left(\bHcheck_{2};\bxihat_{2}\right)}+\frac{1-A_{2}}{2-\pi_{2}\left(\bHcheck_{2};\bxihat_{2}\right)}\right\}.

By Lemma

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1},\mathbf{a}_{1}}\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})\bigg|=

\displaystyle o_{\Pbb}(1),

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{2},\mathbf{a}_{2}}\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(A_{2}=\dhat_{2})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})I(\dbar_{2}=A_{2})\bigg|=

\displaystyle o_{\Pbb}(1),

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\bigg|=

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Using the above and Lemma

we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\bHcheck_{1},A_{1}}\left\{(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})-(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right),

which gives us

By Theorem 2.1 in

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left(\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL_{i};\bThetahat)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}[\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetahat)]\right)-\left(\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL_{i};\bThetabar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}[\nu_{\subSSLDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)]\right)\right\}=o_{\Pbb}(1).

(18)

Proof of Proposition
8
.

Recall the definition of
\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)
in (

), using (

) we have

\text{Bias}\left\{\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bThetabar\right)\right\}=\text{Bias}\left\{\Vbar,\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar,\mubar)\right\}.

Therefore, by Lemma
18
we have

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left\{\Vbar,\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar,\mubar)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\bHcheck_{1}}|\{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxibar_{1})\}^{-1}|}\sqrt{\|\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxibar_{1})-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}\sqrt{\|\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetabar_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\bHcheck_{2}}\left|\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxibar_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\right\}\{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxibar_{1})\}^{-1}\{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxibar_{2})\}^{-1}\right|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sqrt{\|\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxibar_{2})-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}\sqrt{\|\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bthetabar_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}.

Next using Theorem

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\Vhat\subSSLDR-\Vbar\right\}+\sqrt{n}\text{Bias}\left\{\Vbar,\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar,\mubar)\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N\left(0,\sigma\subSSLDR^{2}\right),

(19)

if either (
1
) or (

) are correct then
\text{Bias}\left\{\Vbar,\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar,\mubar)\right\}=o_{\Pbb}(1)
,

we have

\Vhat\subSSLDR-\Vbar\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Pbb}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

which is the required result for Proposition

(a).

Next, if
\sqrt{\|\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxihat_{t})-\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}\sqrt{\|\Qopt_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bthetahat_{t})-\Qopt_{t}(\bHcheck_{t})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right)
