Before proving Proposition
9
, we introduce a useful definition and state the necessary assumption to prove the result. Let
\psi\subSUP^{\xi}(\bL;\bxibar)
and
\psi\subSSL^{\xi}(\bL;\bxibar)
be the supervised and SSL influence functions respectively for
\bxi
, then we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)=

\displaystyle\Vsc\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar,\mubar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]+\mathcal{E}^{\theta}(\bUvec)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\xi}(\bUvec)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\xi}(\bUvec)=

\displaystyle\psi\subSUP^{\xi}(\bL;\bxibar)-\psi\subSSL^{\xi}(\bL;\bxibar).

We need to ensure that the imputation models
\mubar^{v}_{2}(\bUvec),

\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),

\mubar^{v}_{t\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),

t=2,3
used in the SSL value function estimator
V\subSSLDR
are unbiased when multiplied by several functions. For example, we need additional constraints of the type:

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2};\bthetabar_{1})\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\}\right]=

\displaystyle\bzero,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})^{2}\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\}\right]=

\displaystyle\bzero,

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})^{2}\{Y_{2}-\mubar_{2}(\bUvec)\}\right]=

\displaystyle\bzero,

so the imputation models are unbiased in expectation when multiplied by every term and cross-product of terms in

\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)
,

\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)
. These constraints can be summarized in the following Assumption.

{assumption}

Imputation models
\mubar^{v}_{2}(\bUvec),

\mubar^{v}_{\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),

\mubar^{v}_{t\omega_{2}}(\bUvec),

t=2,3
satisfy

\Ebb\left[\left\{\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)-\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)\right]=0.

Proof of Proposition
9
.

From Theorem
19
in Appendix
D.1
we have that the influence function for the fully-supervised value function estimator (
5
) is:

\displaystyle\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)=

\displaystyle\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]+\bpsi\subSUP^{\theta}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bpsi\subSUP^{\xi}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}.

Next, as we estimate
\bxi
with a semi-supervised approach such that
\bpsi^{\xi}\subSSL(\bL;\bxibar)=\bpsi^{\xi}\subSUP(\bL;\bxibar)-\mathcal{E}^{\xi}(\bUvec)
, simple algebra can be used to show that

\psi^{v}\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetabar)=\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)-\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec).

Using the above we can write

\displaystyle\sigma\subSSLDR^{2}=\Ebb\left[\psi^{v}\subSSLDR(\bL;\bThetabar)^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)-\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)^{2}\right]+\Ebb\left[\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\Ebb\left[\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)\right].

By Assumption
B.2
, we have

\Ebb\left[\left\{\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)-\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)\right]=0,

hence

\sigma\subSSLDR^{2}=\sigma\subSUPDR^{2}-\text{Var}\left[\mathcal{E}^{v}(\bUvec)\right].

∎

B.2.1
Variance Estimation for
\Vhat\subSUPDR

As discussed in Remark
10
, to estimate standard errors for
V\subSSLDR(\bUvec;\bThetabar)
, we will approximate the derivatives of the expectation terms
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bTheta}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}
using kernel smoothing to replace the indicator functions. In particular, let
\mathbb{K}_{h}(x)=\frac{1}{h}\sigma(x/h)
, with
\sigma
defined as in (
4
), we approximate
d_{t}(\bH_{t},\btheta_{t})=I(\bH_{t1}\trans\bgamma_{t}>0)
with
\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{t1}\trans\bgamma_{t})

t=1,2
, and define the smoothed propensity score weights as

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{A_{1}\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1})}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{\left\{1-A_{1}\right\}\left\{1-\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1})\right\}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})},\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left[\frac{A_{2}\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2})}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{\left\{1-A_{2}\right\}\left\{1-\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2})\right\}}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right].

For simplicity we’ll set
h=1
, the derivatives are as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bTheta)=\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\btheta_{1})+&\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\right\}\left[Y_{2}-\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1},\btheta_{1})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]\\
+&\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left[-\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1},\btheta_{1})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right]\\
+&\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)\right\}\left[Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right]\\
-&\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2}),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\btheta_{1})&=[\bH_{10}\trans,\bH_{11}\trans I\left(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1}>0\right),\bzero\trans]\trans,\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})&=[\bzero\trans,\bH_{20}\trans,\bH_{21}\trans I\left(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2}>0\right)]\trans,\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)&=\left[\bzero\trans,\bH_{11}\trans\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1})\{1-\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{11}\trans\bgamma_{1})\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}-\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\},\bzero\trans\right]\trans\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)&=\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left\{\frac{A_{2}d_{2}(\bH_{2};\btheta_{2})}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{\left\{1-A_{2}\right\}\left\{1-d_{2}(\bH_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\\
&+\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left[\bzero\trans,\bH_{21}\trans\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2})(1-\mathbb{K}_{h}(\bH_{21}\trans\bgamma_{2}))\left\{\frac{A_{2}}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}-\frac{1-A_{2}}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\right]\trans.\\
\end{split}

Next we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bTheta)=&\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\right\}\left[Y_{2}-\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1},\btheta_{1})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]\\
+&\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)\right\}\left[Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right],\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)&=\left[\varpi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})\trans,\bzero\trans\right]\trans,\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\tilde{\omega}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},\bTheta)&=\left[\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\varpi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})\trans,\bzero\trans\right]\trans,\\
\varpi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})&\equiv\bHcheck_{t1}\left\{-d_{t}(\bHcheck_{t},\btheta_{t})A_{t}\frac{1-\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})}{\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})}+\{1-d_{t}(\bH_{t},\btheta_{t})\}\{1-A_{t}\}\frac{\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})}{1-\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})}\right\}.\end{split}

Appendix C
Technical Lemmas

We start with a simple Lemma that will save us some algebra:

Lemma 14
.

For a fixed
\ell
, let
\bX\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
be a random bounded vector and functions
g_{1}(\bX),\>g_{2}(\bX)
be measurable functions of
\bX
. Let
\mathbb{S}_{n}=\{\bX\}_{i=1}^{n}
be an
i.i.d.
sample, and
\hat{g}_{1}(\cdot)
,
\hat{g}_{2}(\cdot)
be the estimators for functions
g_{1},\>g_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
respectively with
\sup_{\bX}|g_{1}(\bX)|
,
\sup_{\bX}|g_{2}(\bX)|
,
\sup_{\bX}|\hat{g}_{1}(\bX)|
,
\sup_{\bX}|\hat{g}_{2}(\bX)|<\kappa
for fixed
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
. If
\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{k}-g_{k}\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, for
k=1,2,
then
\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}\hat{g}_{2}-g_{1}g_{2}\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
14
.

By definition,
\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}\hat{g}_{2}-g_{1}g_{2}\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
if and only if for a given any
\epsilon>0
,
\exists M_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\Pbb\left(\left|\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}\hat{g}_{2}-g_{1}g_{2}\}\right|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\leq\epsilon

\forall n
.
Let
M_{\epsilon}>0
,

\displaystyle\Pbb\left(|\Pbb_{n}\{g_{1}g_{2}-g_{1}g_{2}\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pbb\left(|\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}\hat{g}_{2}-\hat{g}_{1}g_{2}+\hat{g}_{1}g_{2}-g_{1}g_{2}\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pbb\left(|\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}(\hat{g}_{2}-g_{2})\}|+|\Pbb_{n}\{g_{2}(\hat{g}_{1}-g_{1})\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pbb\left(\sup_{\bX}|\hat{g}_{1}(\bX)||\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{2}-g_{2}\}|+\sup_{\bX}|g_{2}(\bX)||\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}-g_{1}\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

which follows from bounded functions, the union bound, now since
\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{k}(\bX)-g_{k}(\bX)\}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
,
k=1,2
, there exists
M_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\Pbb\left(|\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{2}-g_{2}\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\kappa}\right)+\Pbb\left(|\Pbb_{n}\{\hat{g}_{1}-g_{1}\}|>M_{\epsilon}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\kappa}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}=\epsilon.

∎

Lemma 15
.

(Lemma (A.1) (a) in
Chakrabortty et al. 2018
)

Let
\bX\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
be any random vector and
g(\bX)\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
be any measurable function of
\bX
, with
\ell
and
d
fixed. Let
\mathbb{S}_{n}=\{\bX\}_{i=1}^{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}=\{\bX\}_{j=1}^{N}
be two random samples of
n
and
N

i.i.d
observations of
\bX
respectively, such that
\mathbb{S}_{n}\indep\mathbb{S}_{N}
. Let
\hat{g}_{n}(\cdot)
be any estimator of
g(\cdot)
estimated with
\mathbb{S}_{n}
such that the random sequence:
\hat{T}_{n}=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\|\hat{g}_{n}(\cdot)\|=O_{\Pbb}(1)
, where
\bX\in\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
. Further define the following random sequences:
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n,N}\equiv\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\hat{g}_{n}(\bX_{j})
, and
\bar{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n}\equiv\Ebb_{\mathbb{S}_{N}}\left[\hat{G}_{n,N}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\bX}\left[\hat{g}_{n}(\bX)\right]
, where
\Ebb_{\bX}
is the expectation with respect to
\bX\in\mathbb{S}_{N}
. We assume all expectations involved are finite almost surely (a.s.)
\mathbb{S}_{n}

\forall n
. Then
G_{n,N}-\bar{G}_{n}=O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
.

Proof of lemma
15
.

The following proof follows similar arguments to
Chakrabortty et al. 2018
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{n,N}
,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
be the
j^{th}
element of
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n,N}
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n}
respectively, with
j\in\{1,\dots,\ell\}
. We show that
\mathcal{G}_{n,N}-\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, which implies Lemma
15
for any
\ell
dimensional
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n,N}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{G}}_{n}
. Denote by
\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}
,
\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}}
denote the joint probability distributions of samples
\mathbb{S}_{n}
and
\mathbb{S}_{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}
respectively. Further let
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}[\cdot]
denote the expectation with respect to
\mathbb{S}_{n}
. Since
\mathbb{S}_{n}\indep\mathbb{S}_{N}
using Hoeffding’s inequality

\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{N}}\left(\left|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n,N}-\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\right|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}}t\bigg|\mathbb{S}_{n}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2N^{2}t^{2}}{4N^{2}\hat{T}^{2}_{n}}\right)\text{ a.s. }\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}.

Also, as
\mathbb{S}_{n}\indep\mathbb{S}_{N}
we have

\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}}\left[\left|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n,N}-\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\right|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}}t\right]=\Ebb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left[\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{N}}\left\{\left|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n,N}-\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\right|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}}t\bigg|\mathbb{S}_{n}\right\}\right].

Next, we have that
\hat{T}_{n}=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\|\hat{g}_{n}(\cdot)\|=O_{\Pbb}(1)
and is non-negative, thus
\forall\epsilon>0

\exists\>\delta(\epsilon)>0
such that

\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\hat{T}_{n}>\delta(\epsilon)\right)<\epsilon/4
, using the above we have that
\forall

n,N
:

\displaystyle\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}}\left(\left|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n,N}-\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\right|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}}t\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left[2\exp\left(-\frac{2N^{2}t^{2}}{4N^{2}\hat{T}^{2}_{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left[2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\hat{T}^{2}_{n}}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left[2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\hat{T}^{2}_{n}}\right)\left(I\{\hat{T}_{n}>\delta(\epsilon)\}+I\{\hat{T}_{n}\leq\delta(\epsilon)\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\hat{T}_{n}<\delta(\epsilon)\right)+2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\delta^{2}(\epsilon)}\right)\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\hat{T}_{n}>\delta(\epsilon)\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\delta^{2}(\epsilon)}\right)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{2}=\epsilon,

where the last step follows from choosing
t
large enough such that
\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\delta^{2}(\epsilon)}\right)\leq\epsilon/4.

∎

For Assumption
C
and Lemma
16
we first define some notation and set up the problem. Let
\bX=(\bX_{1},\bX_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}
be any random vector and
g(\bX_{1})\in\mathbb{R}
be any measurable function of
\bX_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{1}}
with
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
fixed. Suppose we’re interested in estimating
m(\bX_{2})=\mathbb{E}[g(\bX_{1})|\bX_{2}]
. Let
\mathbb{S}_{n}=\{\bX\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a random sample of
n
i.i.d. observations of
\bX
, and
\mathbb{S}_{k=1}^{K}
denote a random partition of
\mathbb{S}_{n}
into
K
disjoint subsets of size
n_{K}=\frac{n}{K}
with index sets
\{\mathcal{I}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
. We will use cross-validation to estimate
\mhat(\bX_{2})
, that is, we use subset
\mathcal{I}_{k}
to train estimator
\mhat_{k}
and we estimate
m(\bX_{2})
with:
\mhat(\bX_{2})=K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\mhat_{k}(\bX_{2})
,
K\geq 2
. Denote by
\hat{C}_{n,N}\in\mathbb{R}
an estimator which depends on both samples
\mathbb{S}_{n},\mathbb{S}_{N}
. Additionally, let function
\hat{\pi}_{n}(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{2}}\rightarrow(0,1)
be a random function with limit
\pi(\cdot)
,
\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2}):\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{2}}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
, be a random function with limit
l(\bX_{2})
, and finally function
f:\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
d\leq\ell_{2}
be any deterministic function of
\bX_{2}
.

{assumption}

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
for an arbitrary
p\in\mathbb{N}
i) function
w:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and estimator
\hat{\pi}_{n}
are such that
\sup_{\bX_{2}}\left|\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2})^{-1}-\pi(\bX_{2})^{-1}\right|=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, ii) function
l:\mathcal{X}\mapsto\{0,1\}
and estimator
\hat{l}_{n}
are such that
\sup_{\bX_{2}}\left|\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2})-l(\bX_{2})\right|=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, and iii) function
f:\mathbb{R}^{\ell_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
d\leq\ell_{2}
is such that
\sup_{\bX_{2}}\|f(\bX_{2})\|<\infty
.

Lemma 16
.

Define
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}^{n}_{k}(\bX_{2})=\hat{C}_{n,N}\frac{\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2})}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2})}f(\bX_{2})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{l(\bX_{2})}{\pi(\bX_{2})}f(\boldsymbol{x}_{2})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2})\right]
for
\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2})=\mhat_{k}(\bX_{2})-m(\bX_{2})
, and
\hat{C}_{n,N}\in\mathbb{R}
which satisfies
\hat{C}=1+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Under Assumptions
5.2
and
C
, there is
c_{n_{K}^{-}}=o(1)
such that
\mathbb{G}_{n,K}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}^{n}_{k}(\bX_{2})=O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)
,

Proof of Lemma
16
.

First we define

\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})-\Ebb\left[\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\right],

for any sample subset
\mathbb{S}_{K}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
, let
\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{K}}
denote the joint probability distribution of
\mathbb{S}_{K}
, and let
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}_{K}}[\cdot]
denote expectation with respect to
\Pbb_{\mathbb{S}_{K}}
, and
\mathbb{G}_{n,K}=K^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{(n)}
, Next by Assumption
5.2
we have
\dhat_{k}\equiv\sup_{\bX_{2}}\hat{\Delta}(\bX_{2})=o_{\Pbb}(1)
. Finally let
B_{1}=\sup_{\bX_{2}}\|f(\bX_{2})\|_{2}<\infty
,
B_{2}<\infty
be the upperbound to
\sup_{\bX_{2}}|\pi(\bX_{2})^{-1}|,\sup_{\bX_{2}}|\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2})|\sup_{\bX_{2}}|\frac{\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2})}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2})}|
.

First note that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{G}_{n,K}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\hat{C}_{n,N}\frac{\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2i})}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\hat{C}_{n,N}-1\right)n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\frac{\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2i})}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2i})}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2i})\left(\frac{1}{\hat{\pi}_{n}(\bX_{2i})}-\frac{1}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\frac{1}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}\left(\hat{l}_{n}(\bX_{2i})-l(\bX_{2i})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\right]\right\|_{2},

which follows from the triangle inequality, next as
f(\cdot),\hat{\pi}_{n}(\cdot)^{-1},\pi(\cdot)^{-1},\hat{l}_{n}(\cdot)
are bounded
\forall\bX_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
, and using uniform bounds of
O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
for the difference terms we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{G}_{n,K}\right\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)n^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}B_{2}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\dhat_{k}\right|+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)n^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}B_{2}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\dhat_{k}\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)n^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}B_{2}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\dhat_{k}\right|+\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2},

\displaystyle\leq\left\|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\right]\right\|_{2}+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right).

where the last step follows from
\dhat_{k}=o_{\Pbb}(1)
.
Next we want to bound the first term above by
c_{n_{K}^{-}}
in probability, note that
\forall\epsilon

\exists M>0
such that

\displaystyle\Pbb\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2}>Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)\leq\Pbb\left(K^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2}>Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Pbb\left(\left\|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2}>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{K^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Pbb\left(\left|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\right|>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{(Kd)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left[\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}}\left(\left|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\right|>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{(Kd)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\biggr|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}\right)\right],

where the first 3 steps follow from applying Boole’s inequality and the triangle inequality, the fourth step follows from iterated expectations for the the event
\left\{\left|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\right|>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{(Kd)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}
.

Next, we have
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}\indep\mathcal{L}_{k}
,
\forall\>k\in\{1,\dots,K\}
, thus conditional on
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}
,
n^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}
is a sum of iid centered random vectors
\left\{\frac{l(\bX_{2i})}{\pi(\bX_{2i})}f(\bX_{2i})\hat{\Delta}_{k}(\bX_{2i})\right\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}
which are bounded a.s.
\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}}^{-},\>\forall k,n.
Thus we can apply Hoeffding’s inequality to
\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\forall j
:

\displaystyle\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}}\left(\left|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\right|>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{(Kd)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\biggr|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{M^{2}c_{n_{K}^{-}}^{2}}{2KdB^{2}\dhat_{k}^{2}}\right\}

(20)

a.s.
\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\forall n;
and for each
k\in\{1,\dots,K\},j\in\{1\dots,d\}.
Note that
\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\geq 0
is stochastically bounded away from zero as
\dhat_{k}=o_{\Pbb}(1)
, therefore
\forall k
and given
\epsilon>0
,
\exists\delta(\epsilon,k)>0
such that
\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\leq\delta(\epsilon,k)\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4Kd}
, let
\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)=\min_{k}\{\delta(\epsilon,k)\}
, we have that

\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\leq\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4Kd}
.

Therefore using the bound in (
20
) and event
\left\{\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\leq\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right\}
:

\displaystyle\Pbb\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2}>Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left[\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}}\left(\left|\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k[j]}\right|>\frac{Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}}{(Kd)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\biggr|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left[2\exp\left\{-\frac{M^{2}c_{n_{K}^{-}}^{2}}{2KdB^{2}\dhat_{k}^{2}}\right\}\left(I\left\{\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\leq\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right\}+I\left\{\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2{K}d\exp\left\{-\frac{M^{2}\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)^{2}}{2KdB^{2}}\right\}\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}\leq\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right)+2{K}d\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}>\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{K}d\frac{\epsilon}{4Kd}+2{K}d\exp\left\{-\frac{M^{2}\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)^{2}}{2KdB^{2}}\right\}\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}>\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\right),

next note that choosing a large enough
M
such that

\exp\left\{-\frac{M^{2}\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)^{2}}{2KdB^{2}}\right\}<\frac{\epsilon}{4Kd}
, since
\Pbb_{\mathcal{L}_{k}^{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n_{K}^{-}}}{D_{k}}>\delta^{*}(\epsilon,k)\leq 1\right)
we get
\Pbb\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{G}^{(n)}_{k}\right\|_{2}>Mc_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}=\epsilon
.

Finally we have

\mathbb{G}_{n,K}=O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n_{K}^{-}}\right)+o_{\Pbb}(1)=O_{\Pbb}\left(c_{n_{K}^{-}}\right).

∎

Lemma 17
.

Let
\bgammahat\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random variable such that
\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat-\bgammabar\right)=O_{\Pbb}(1)
, then for any fixed vector
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have that (a)
\sqrt{n}\left(\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat\right]_{+}-\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar\right]_{+}\right)=\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat-\bgammabar\right)I(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar>0)+o_{\Pbb}(1)
, (b) Functions
\dhat_{t}

t=1,2,
defined in Section
4
and propensity scores
\pi_{1}
in (
4
) satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1},\mathbf{a}_{1}}\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})\bigg|=

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{2},\mathbf{a}_{2}}\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(A_{2}=\dhat_{2})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})I(\dbar_{2}=A_{2})\bigg|=

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\bigg|=

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(c) For
\bthetahat
,
\bxihat
estimated via our semi-supervised approach, and limits
\bthetabar
,
\bxibar
defined in Assumptions
5.1
and
5.2
respectively

\hat{C}^{(1)}_{n,N}=\frac{(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}}{(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{n}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})\right\}},\>\hat{C}^{(2)}_{n,N}=\frac{\Pbb_{N}\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2},A_{2};\bthetabar_{2})\right\}}{\Pbb_{n}\left\{\Qopt_{2-}(\bH_{2},A_{2};\bthetahat_{2})\right\}},

satisfy
\hat{C}^{(1)}_{n,N}=1+O_{\Pbb}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
,
\hat{C}^{(2)}_{n,N}=1+O_{\Pbb}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Proof of Lemma
17
.

Define set
\mathcal{A}_{q}
for any
q
dimensional vector
\boldsymbol{\hat{\gamma}}
as

\mathcal{A}_{q}=\left\{\bgammahat\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\>\bigg|\>\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar<\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat<2\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar,\>\>\forall\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\right\}.

Now consider
\bgammahat\in\mathcal{A}_{q}:

•

if sign
(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar)=1
, then
0<\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar<\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat\implies
sign
(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat)=1
,

•

if sign
(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar)=-1
, then
\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat<2\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar<0\implies
sign
(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat)=-1
.

Assuming
\sqrt{n}(\bgammahat-\bgammabar)=O_{\Pbb}(1)
,
\mathcal{A}_{q}
exists and in fact it is such that
\Pbb\left(\bgammahat\in\mathcal{A}_{q}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
.

(a) Using the above:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat\right]_{+}-\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar\right]_{+}\right)=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat-\bgammabar\right)I(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar>0)I\left(\bgammahat\in\mathcal{A}_{q}\right)+\sqrt{n}\left(\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammahat\right]_{+}-\left[\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar\right]_{+}\right)I\left(\bgammahat\notin\mathcal{A}_{q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\bgammahat-\bgammabar\right)I(\mathbf{a}\trans\bgammabar>0)+o_{\Pbb}(1).

(b) As
A_{ti}\in\{0,1\}
,
t=1,2,
we can write

\displaystyle I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(\dhat_{2}=A_{2})=

\displaystyle I\left\{A_{1}=I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)\right\}I\left\{A_{2}=I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I\{A_{1}=I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)\}I\{A_{2}=I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{2}I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})(1-A_{2})I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle A_{1}(1-A_{2})I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})A_{2}I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0),

therefore

\displaystyle\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(\dhat_{2}=A_{2})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})I(\dbar_{2}=A_{2})\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|A_{1}A_{2}\left\{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})(1-A_{2})\left\{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}<0)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle A_{1}(1-A_{2})\left\{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}<0)\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})A_{2}\left\{I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\right\}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{2}\bigg|I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\bigg|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})(1-A_{2})\bigg|I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}<0)\bigg|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle A_{1}(1-A_{2})\bigg|I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}<0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}>0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}<0)\bigg|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(1-A_{1})A_{2}\bigg|I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\bigg|

where the first step follows from above, the second step from the triangle inequality, now as
\bgammahat_{1}
,
\bgammahat_{2}
have dimensions
q_{12},\>q_{22}
respectively, we use sets
\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}}
,
\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}}
and have

\displaystyle\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(\dhat_{2}=A_{2})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})I(\dbar_{2}=A_{2})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{2}I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})+(1-A_{1})(1-A_{2})I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle A_{1}(1-A_{2})I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})+(1-A_{1})A_{2}I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})

which follows from the fact that for any term within absolute value:

\bigg|I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)-I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\bigg|=I(\bgammahat_{1}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}})I(\bgammahat_{2}\notin\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}})

since for
I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}>0)\neq I(\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1}<0)I(\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}>0)
both
\bgammahat_{1},\bgammahat_{2}
have to be outside sets
\mathcal{A}_{q_{12}},\mathcal{A}_{q_{22}}
respectively. Thus

\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})I(\dhat_{2}=A_{2})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})I(\dbar_{2}=A_{2})\bigg|=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

we can analogous show that
\bigg|I(\dhat_{1}=A_{1})-I(\dbar_{1}=A_{1})\bigg|=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\forall i
.

Next to see
\sup_{\bH_{1}}\left|\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\right|=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, note that as
\mathcal{H}_{1}
,
\Omega_{1}
are bounded sets we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1}}\left|\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\right|=\sup_{\bH_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}}\left|e^{-\bH_{1}\trans\hat{\bxi_{1}}}-e^{-\bH_{1}\trans\bxibar_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bH_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1},\bxi_{1}\in\Omega_{1}}\left|\frac{d}{dx}e^{-x}\big|_{x=\bH_{1}\trans\bxi_{1}}\right|\sup_{\bH_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}}\left|\bH_{1}\trans\hat{\bxi_{1}}-\bH_{1}\trans\bxibar\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bH_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1},\bxi_{1}\in\Omega_{1}}\left|\frac{d}{dx}e^{-x}\big|_{x=\bH_{1}\trans\bxi_{1}}\right|\sup_{\bH_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}}\|\bH_{1}\|\left\|\hat{\bxi}_{1}-\bxibar_{1}\right\|_{2}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

where we use the definition of
\pi_{1}
in (
4
), Lipschitz and
\left\|\hat{\bxi}_{1}-\bxibar_{1}\right\|_{2}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
from Assumptions (
5.2
) and Theorem 5.21 in
Vaart 1998
as we are using Z-estimation for
\bxi_{1}
.

(c) By Theorem
2
we have
\hat{\beta}_{21}-\bar{\beta}_{21}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Next, we can write

\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})=I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)\right\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)}\right\}.

By Lemma
17
(b) it follows that

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\left[I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1}\right)\right\}-I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\left[\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pbb_{n}\left[\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1})}-\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Using the above and Lemma
14
we get

(1+\hat{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{n}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1})\right\}=(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{n}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

Also by CLT we have

\displaystyle(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{n}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle=(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Ebb\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Pbb_{N}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle=(1+\bar{\beta}_{21})\Ebb\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}+O_{\Pbb}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

finally by Slutsky’s theorem

\hat{C}_{n,N}^{(1)}-1=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).
With similar arguments, and using Lemma
17
(a) to see
\Pbb_{n}\left(\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2}\right]_{+}-\left[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}\right]_{+}\right)=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, we can show
\hat{C}_{n,N}^{(2)}-1=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

∎

Lemma 18
.

Let
Q_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\btheta_{t})
,
\pi_{t}(\bHcheck_{t};\bxi_{t})

t=1,2
be estimator functions of (
1
) & (
4
) respectively and define the bias as
Bias\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)\equiv\Vbar-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right]
, then

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{1-\frac{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{1-\frac{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{1-\frac{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right].

where
\Vbar=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bH_{2},Y_{2},A_{2}=\dbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})]|\bH_{1},A_{1}=\dbar_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})]]
is the mean population value under the optimal treatment rule.

Proof of Lemma
18
.

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[Y_{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bH_{2},Y_{2},A_{2}=\dbar_{2}]|\bH_{1},A_{1}=\dbar_{1}]]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\left\{Y_{2}-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bTheta_{2})\left\{Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right].

Adding and subtracting
\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bH_{2},\dbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2}),Y_{2}]\right]
,

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bH_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\bigg\{Y_{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bH_{2}\dbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2}),Y_{2}]-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\bigg\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bTheta_{2})\left\{Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right],

using iterated expectations in the second and fourth terms:

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\left\{Y_{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bHcheck_{2},\dbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2}),Y_{2}]-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\bigg|\bHcheck_{1},A_{1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bTheta_{2})\left\{Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\bigg|\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},Y_{2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y_{3}|\bHcheck_{2},\dbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2}),Y_{2}]\bigg|\bHcheck_{1},A_{1}\right]-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta_{1})\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\bTheta_{2})\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{3}|\bHcheck_{2},A_{2},Y_{2}\right]-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right].

using definitions of
\omega_{t}(\bHcheck_{t},A_{t};\bTheta_{t})

t=1,2
we can write:

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\frac{\dbar_{2}A_{2}}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{2})(1-A_{2})}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

assuming
A_{1}\perp A_{2}|\bH_{2},Y_{2}
, we use iterated expectations:

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\bigg|\bHcheck_{1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\frac{\dbar_{2}A_{2}}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{2})(1-A_{2})}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\bigg|\bHcheck_{2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{\{1-\dbar_{1}\}\{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})\}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Ebb\left[\left\{\frac{\dbar_{1}A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{(1-\dbar_{1})(1-A_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\frac{\dbar_{2}\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}+\frac{\{1-\dbar_{2}\}\{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\}}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

finally, factorizing common terms:

\displaystyle\text{Bias}\left(\Vbar,\mathcal{V}\subSUPDR\left(\bL;\bTheta\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\dbar_{1}\left\{1-\frac{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\{1-\dbar_{1}\}\left\{1-\frac{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\dbar_{2}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{1-\frac{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})}{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\{1-\dbar_{2}\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\left\{1-\frac{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})}{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})}\right\}\left\{\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\bHcheck_{1}}|\{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})\}^{-1}|}\sqrt{\|\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}\sqrt{\|\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bthetahat_{1})-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\bHcheck_{2}}\left|\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})}\right\}\{1-\pi_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\bxi_{1})\}^{-1}\{1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})\}^{-1}\right|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sqrt{\|\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}}\sqrt{\|\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bthetahat_{2})-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2})\|_{L_{2}(\Pbb)}},

which follows by Cauchy–Schwarz Inequality.
∎

Appendix D
Additional Theoretical Results

D.1
Augmented value function estimation

We first re-write Assumption
5.2
to account for only using sample
\mathcal{L}
in estimation of the
Q
functions and propensity scores.

{assumption}

Define the following class of functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv\left\{Q_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\btheta_{1})|\btheta_{1}\in\Theta_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{q_{1}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\left\{Q_{2}(\bH_{2},A_{2},Y_{2};\btheta_{2})|\btheta_{2}\in\Theta_{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{q_{2}}\right\},

(21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv\left\{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxi_{1})|\bxi_{1}\in\Omega_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\left\{\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})|\bxi_{2}\in\Omega_{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}\right\},

with
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}
fixed under model definitions (
1
) & (
4
). Let the population equations
\mathbb{E}\left[S^{\xi}_{t}(\bxi_{t})\right]=\bzero,t=1,2
have solutions
\bxibar_{1},\bxibar_{2}
, where

\displaystyle S^{\xi}_{1}(\bxi_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi_{1}}\log\left[\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxi_{1})^{A_{1}}(1-\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxi_{1}))^{(1-A_{1})}\right],

\displaystyle S^{\xi}_{2}(\bxi_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi_{2}}\log\left[\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2})^{A_{2}}(1-\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxi_{2}))^{(1-A_{2})}\right],

and the population equations for the
Q
functions
\mathbb{E}[S^{\theta}_{t}(\btheta_{t})]=\bzero,t=1,2
have solutions
\bthetabar_{1},\bthetabar_{2}
, where

\displaystyle S^{\theta}_{2}(\btheta_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta_{2}\trans}\|Y_{3}-Q_{2}(\bHcheck_{2},A_{2};\btheta_{2})\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle S^{\theta}_{1}(\btheta_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta_{1}\trans}\|Y_{2}+\Qbar_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bthetabar_{2})-Q_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\btheta_{1})\|_{2}^{2},

(i)
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
are bounded sets. (ii)
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
are open bounded sets and for some
r>0
and
g_{t}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\bigg|Q_{t}(\cdot;\btheta_{t})-Q_{t}(\cdot;\btheta_{t}^{\prime})\bigg|\leq g_{t}(\cdot)\|\btheta_{t}-\btheta_{t}^{\prime}\|\>\>\forall\btheta_{t},\btheta_{t}^{\prime}\in\Theta_{t},\>\mathbb{E}\left[|g_{t}(\cdot)|^{r}\right]<\infty,\>t=1,2.

(22)

(iii) The population minimizers satisfy
\bthetabar_{t}\in\Theta_{t},\bxibar_{t}\in\Omega_{t},t=1,2
.
(iv) For
\bxibar_{t},t=1,2
,
\bar{\pi}_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})>0,\bar{\pi}_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxibar_{2})>0\>\forall\bH\in\mathcal{H}
.

Existence of solutions
\bthetabar_{t}\in\Theta_{t}
,
t=1,2
is clear as
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}
are open and bounded.

Theorem 19

(Asymptotic Normality for
\Vhat\subSSLDR
)
.

Under Assumptions
5.1
,
5.1
, and
D.1
,
\Vhat\subSUPDR
as defined in (
5
) is such that

\sqrt{n}\left\{\Vhat\subSUPDR-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]\right\}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi^{v}_{\subSUPDR}(\bL_{i};\bThetabar)+o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}N\left(0,\sigma\subSUPDR^{2}\right).

where

\displaystyle\psi^{v}\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)=

\displaystyle\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{S}}\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]+\bpsi\subSUP^{\theta}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar}

\displaystyle+\bpsi\subSUP^{\xi}(\bL)\trans\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)d\Pbb_{\bL}\bigg|_{\bTheta=\bThetabar},

\displaystyle\sigma\subSUPDR^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\psi^{v}_{\subSUPDR}(\bL;\bThetabar)^{2}\right].

proof of theorem
19
.

Letting
g(\bTheta)=\int\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bTheta)d\Pbb_{\bL}
, we start by centering (
5
) and scaling by
\sqrt{n}
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{\Pbb_{n}\left(\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}+\sqrt{n}\left\{g(\bThetahat\subSUP)-g(\bThetabar)\right\}

I) Empirical Process Term

We first show that under Assumption
D.1
,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}=o_{\Pbb}(1)
, let

\displaystyle f_{\bTheta}(\bUvec)=\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})+

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1},\bTheta)\left\{Y_{2}-\Qopt_{1}(\bHcheck_{1};\btheta_{1})+\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\omega_{2}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bTheta)\left\{Y_{3}-\Qopt_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\btheta_{2})\right\},

we define the class of functions
\mathcal{C}_{3}=\left\{f_{\bTheta}(\bUvec)|\bUvec,\bTheta\in\mathcal{S}(\delta)\right\},
and

\ell=\{l:\{0,1\}^{2}\mapsto\{0,1\}\}.

i) By Assumptions
D.1
and
Theorem 19.5 in
Vaart 1998
,
\ell,\>\mathcal{W}_{t},\>\mathcal{Q}_{t},t=1,2
are a
\Pbb
-Donsker class, thus it follows that
\mathcal{C}_{3}
is a Donsker class.

ii) We estimate
\bxi_{1},\bxi_{2}
from (
D.1
) with their maximum likelihood estimator
\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP},\bxihat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
, solving
\Pbb_{n}\left[S_{t}(\bxi_{t})\right]=\bzero,t=1,2
and estimate functions
\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}),

\pi_{2}(\bHcheck_{2};\bxihat_{2\subSUP})
with
\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP},\bxihat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
. By Assumption
D.1
and weak law of large numbers
\bxihat_{t\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bxibar_{t},t=1,2
.

Analogous, under regularity conditions (
) and (
) have unique solutions
\bthetahat_{t\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}
for which
\bthetahat_{t\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bthetabar_{t},t=1,2
by Assumption
D.1
and weak law of large numbers. Both regardless of whether models (
1
) & (
4
) are correct.
Thus
\Pbb\left(\bThetahat\subSUP\in\mathcal{S}(\delta)\right)\rightarrow 1,\>\forall\delta.

iii) We next show
\int\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bL}\longrightarrow 0.

Using (
7
), for a large enough constant
c
we can write

\displaystyle\int\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bL}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bH_{1}}\left(\bH_{10}\trans\bbetabar_{1}+[\bH_{11}\trans\bgammabar_{1}]_{+}-\bH_{10}\trans\bbetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-[\bH_{11}\trans\bgammahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}]_{+}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bHcheck_{2}}\left(\bHcheck_{20}\trans\bbetabar_{2}+[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammabar_{2}]_{+}-\bHcheck_{20}\trans\bbetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-[\bH_{21}\trans\bgammahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}]_{+}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sup_{\bH_{1},A_{1}}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP})-\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}^{2}+\left(\hat{\beta}_{21\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-\bar{\beta}_{21}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0

where we use
(a-b)^{2},(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}\>\forall a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, boundedness of
\bThetabar
and covariates by Assumptions
5.1
,
5.1
, and
D.1
. Next,

from assumption (
7
) it can be shown that
\bthetahat_{2\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-\bthetabar_{2}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
,
\bthetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-\bthetabar_{1}=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, also from Lemma
17
(a) it follows that for
t=1,2

\displaystyle\sup_{\bHcheck_{t}}\left(\bH_{t0}\trans\bbetabar_{t}+[\bH_{t1}\trans\bgammabar_{t}]_{+}-\bH_{t0}\trans\bbetahat_{t\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}-[\bH_{t1}\trans\bgammahat_{t\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}]_{+}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\bHcheck_{t0}}\|\bHcheck_{t0}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bbetahat_{t}-\bbetabar_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+2\sup_{\bH_{t1}}\|\bH_{t1}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bgammahat_{t}-\bgammabar_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-1}\right).

Next, we can write

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(\bH_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP})=

\displaystyle I\left\{A_{1}=d_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\right)\right\}\left\{\frac{A_{1}}{\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\right)}+\frac{1-A_{1}}{1-\pi_{1}\left(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP}\right)}\right\}.

By Lemma
17
(b) it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bH_{1}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxihat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP})}-\frac{1}{\pi_{1}(\bH_{1};\bxibar_{1})}\bigg|=

\displaystyle O_{\Pbb}\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Using the above and Lemma
14
we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\bHcheck_{1},A_{1}}\left\{\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetahat_{1\scriptscriptstyle\sf SUP})-\omega_{1}(\bHcheck_{1},A_{1};\bThetabar_{1})\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=o_{\Pbb}\left(1\right),

which gives us
\int\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bL}\longrightarrow 0.

Hence, we have i)
\Pbb\left(\bThetahat\subSUP\in\mathcal{S}(\delta)\right)\rightarrow 1,\>\forall\delta,
ii)
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is a Donsker class, and

iii)
\int\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}^{2}d\Pbb_{\bL}\longrightarrow 0
, then by Theorem 2.1 in
Van Der Vaart and Wellner 2007

\sqrt{n}\left[\Pbb_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetahat\subSUP)-g(\bThetahat\subSUP)\right\}-\Pbb_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)-g(\bThetabar)\right\}\right]=o_{\Pbb}(1).

Centered Sample Average

Next we consider
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}
. Note that
\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)
is a deterministic function of random variable
\bL
as parameters are fixed. We have that
\Ebb\left[\left(\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar\right)^{2}\right]<\infty
holds by Assumption
5.1
&
D.1
. Thus the central limit theorem yields

\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,Var\left[\Vsc\subSUPDR(\bL;\bThetabar)\right]\right).

Using the

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left\{g(\bThetahat\subSUP)-g(\bThetabar)\right\}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\btheta\subSUP}g(\bThetabar)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi\subSUP^{\theta}(\bL_{i})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\bxi}g(\bThetabar)\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bpsi\subSUP^{\xi}(\bL_{i})+o_{\Pbb}(1)
